> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_494) Unless otherwise specified, each function, and each specialization of any function template, specified in this header is a designated addressable function (\[namespace.std\]).
>
> [2](#pnum_495) The behavior of any function specified in namespace `std``::``meta` is implementation-defined when a reflection of a construct not otherwise specified by this document is provided as an argument.
>
> \[ *Note 1:* Values of type `std``::``meta``::``info` can represent implementation-specific constructs (\[basic.fundamental\]). — *end note* \]
>
> \[ *Note 2:* The behavior of many of the functions specified in namespace `std``::``meta` have semantics that can be affected by the completeness of class types represented by reflection values. For such functions, for any reflection `r` such that `dealias``(``r``)` represents a specialization of a templated class with a reachable definition, the specialization is implicitly instantiated (\[temp.inst\]).
>
> \[ *Example 1:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <class T>
> struct X {
>   T mem;
> };
>
> static_assert(size_of(^^X<int>) == sizeof(int)); // instantiates X<int>
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> — *end note* \]
>
> [3](#pnum_496) Any function in namespace `std``::``meta` whose return type is `string_view` or `u8string_view` returns an object *`V`* such that *`V`*`.``data``()[`*`V`*`.``size``()]` equals `'``\0``'`.
>
> \[ *Example 2:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> struct C { };
>
> constexpr string_view sv = identifier_of(^^C);
> static_assert(sv == "C");
> static_assert(sv.data()[0] == 'C');
> static_assert(sv.data()[1] == '\0');
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> [4](#pnum_497) For the purpose of exposition, throughout this clause `^^`*`E`* is used to indicate a reflection representing source construct *`E`*.

### \[meta.reflection.operators\] Operator representations[](#meta.reflection.operators-operator-representations)

> ``` sourceCode
> enum class operators {
>   see below;
> };
> using enum operators;
> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_498) The enumeration type `operators` specifies constants used to identify operators that can be overloaded, with the meanings listed in Table 1. The values of the constants are distinct.
>
> \[ Drafting note: The names here are chosen after the punctuation marks, not the semantic operation, and we are sticking with the Unicode names — or resorting to the secondary name when the primary name is not well known (e.g. `solidus` -\> `slash`) \]
>
> Table 1: Enum class `operators` \[meta.reflection.operators\]
>
> [TABLE]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval operators operator_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [2](#pnum_499) *Constant When*: `r` represents an operator function or operator function template.
>
> [3](#pnum_500) *Returns*: The value of the enumerator from `operators` whose corresponding *`operator-function-id`* is the unqualified name of the entity represented by `r`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval string_view symbol_of(operators op);
> consteval u8string_view u8symbol_of(operators op);
> ```
>
> [4](#pnum_501) *Constant When*: The value of `op` corresponds to one of the enumerators in `operators`.
>
> [5](#pnum_502) *Returns*: A `string_view` or `u8string_view` containing the characters of the operator symbol name corresponding to `op`, respectively encoded with the ordinary literal encoding or with UTF-8.

### \[meta.reflection.names\] Reflection names and locations[](#meta.reflection.names-reflection-names-and-locations)

> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool has_identifier(info r);
> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_503) *Returns*:
>
> - [(1.1)](#pnum_504) If `r` represents an entity that has a typedef name for linkage purposes (\[dcl.typedef\]), then `true`.
> - [(1.2)](#pnum_505) Otherwise, if `r` represents an unnamed entity, then `false`.
> - [(1.3)](#pnum_506) Otherwise, if `r` represents a class type, then `!``has_template_arguments``(``r``)`.
> - [(1.4)](#pnum_507) Otherwise, if `r` represents a function, then `true` if `has_template_arguments``(``r``)` is `false` and the function is not a constructor, destructor, operator function, or conversion function. Otherwise, `false`.
> - [(1.5)](#pnum_508) Otherwise, if `r` represents a template, then `true` if `r` does not represent a constructor template, operator function template, or conversion function template. Otherwise, `false`.
> - [(1.6)](#pnum_509) Otherwise, if `r` represents a variable, then `false` if the declaration of that variable was instantiated from a function parameter pack. Otherwise, `!``has_template_arguments``(``r``)`.
> - [(1.7)](#pnum_510) Otherwise, if `r` represents a structured binding, then `false` if the declaration of that structured binding was instantiated from a structured binding pack. Otherwise, `true`.
> - [(1.8)](#pnum_511) Otherwise, if `r` represents a type alias, then `!``has_template_arguments``(``r``)`.
> - [(1.9)](#pnum_512) Otherwise, if `r` represents an enumerator, non-static data member, namespace, or namespace alias, then `true`.
> - [(1.10)](#pnum_513) Otherwise, if `r` represents a direct base class relationship, then `has_identifier``(``type_of``(``r``))`.
> - [(1.11)](#pnum_514) Otherwise, `r` represents a data member description (*`T`*, *`N`*, *`A`*, *`W`*, *`NUA`*) (\[class.mem.general\]); `true` if *`N`* is not ⊥. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval string_view identifier_of(info r);
> consteval u8string_view u8identifier_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [2](#pnum_515) Let *E* be UTF-8 for `u8identifier_of`, and otherwise the ordinary literal encoding.
>
> [3](#pnum_516) *Constant When*: `has_identifier``(``r``)` is `true` and the identifier that would be returned (see below) is representable by *`E`*.
>
> [4](#pnum_517) *Returns*: An NTMBS, encoded with *`E`*, determined as follows:
>
> - [(4.1)](#pnum_518) If `r` represents an entity with a typedef name for linkage purposes, then that name.
> - [(4.2)](#pnum_519) Otherwise, if `r` represents a literal operator or literal operator template, then the *`ud-suffix`* of the operator or operator template.
> - [(4.3)](#pnum_520) Otherwise, if `r` represents an entity, then the identifier introduced by the declaration of that entity.
> - [(4.4)](#pnum_521) Otherwise, if `r` represents a direct base class relationship, then `identifier_of``(``type_of``(``r``))` or `u8identifier_of``(``type_of``(``r``))`, respectively.
> - [(4.5)](#pnum_522) Otherwise, `r` represents a data member description (*`T`*, *`N`*, *`A`*, *`W`*, *`NUA`*) (\[class.mem.general\]); a `string_view` or `u8string_view`, respectively, containing the identifier *`N`*.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval string_view display_string_of(info r);
> consteval u8string_view u8display_string_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [5](#pnum_523) *Returns*: An implementation-defined `string_view` or `u8string_view`, respectively.
>
> [6](#pnum_524) *Recommended practice*: Where possible, implementations should return a string suitable for identifying the represented construct.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval source_location source_location_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [7](#pnum_525) *Returns*: If `r` represents a value, a type other than a class type or an enumeration type, the global namespace, or a data member description, then `source_location``{}`. Otherwise, an implementation-defined `source_location` value.
>
> [8](#pnum_526) *Recommended practice*: If `r` represents an entity with a definition that is reachable from the evaluation context, a value corresponding to a definition should be returned.

### \[meta.reflection.queries\] Reflection queries[](#meta.reflection.queries-reflection-queries)

> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool has-type(info r); // exposition only
> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_527) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a value, object, variable, function whose type does not contain an undeduced placeholder type and that is not a constructor or destructor, enumerator, non-static data member, unnamed bit-field, direct base class relationship, or data member description. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval info type_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [2](#pnum_528) *Constant When*: *`has-type`*`(``r``)` is `true`.
>
> [3](#pnum_529) *Returns*:
>
> - [(3.1)](#pnum_530) If `r` represents a value, object, variable, function, non-static data member, or unnamed bit-field, then the type of what is represented by `r`.
> - [(3.2)](#pnum_531) Otherwise, if `r` represents an enumerator *`N`* of an enumeration *`E`*, then:
>   - [(3.2.1)](#pnum_532) If *`E`* is defined by a declaration *`D`* that precedes a point *`P`* in the evaluation context and *`P`* does not occur within an *`enum-specifier`* of *`D`*, then a reflection of *`E`*.
>   - [(3.2.2)](#pnum_533) Otherwise, a reflection of the type of *`N`* prior to the closing brace of the *`enum-specifier`* as specified in \[dcl.enum\].
> - [(3.3)](#pnum_534) Otherwise, if `r` represents a direct base class relationship (*`D`*, *`B`*), then a reflection of *`B`*.
> - [(3.4)](#pnum_535) Otherwise, for a data member description (*`T`*, *`N`*, *`A`*, *`W`*, *`NUA`*) (\[class.mem.general\]), a reflection of the type *`T`*.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval info object_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [4](#pnum_536) *Constant When*: `r` is a reflection representing either
>
> - [(4.1)](#pnum_537) an object with static storage duration (\[basic.stc.general\]), or
> - [(4.2)](#pnum_538) a variable that either declares or refers to such an object, and if that variable is a reference *`R`* then either
>   - [(4.2.1)](#pnum_539) *`R`* is usable in constant expressions (\[expr.const\]), or
>   - [(4.2.2)](#pnum_540) the lifetime of *`R`* began within the core constant expression currently under evaluation.
>
> [5](#pnum_541) *Returns*:
>
> - [(5.1)](#pnum_542) If `r` represents an object, then `r`.
> - [(5.2)](#pnum_543) Otherwise, if `r` represents a reference, then a reflection of the object referred to by that reference.
> - [(5.3)](#pnum_544) Otherwise, `r` represents a variable; a reflection of the object declared by that variable.
>
> \[ *Example 1:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> int x;
> int& y = x;
>
> static_assert(^^x != ^^y);                       // OK, x and y are different variables so their
>                                                  // reflections compare different
> static_assert(object_of(^^x) == object_of(^^y)); // OK, because y is a reference
>                                                  // to x, their underlying objects are the same
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval info constant_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [6](#pnum_545) Let *`R`* be a constant expression of type `info` such that *`R`*` ``==`` r` is `true`.
>
> [7](#pnum_546) *Constant When*: `[:`` `*`R`*` ``:]` is a valid *`splice-expression`* (\[expr.prim.splice\]).
>
> [8](#pnum_547) *Effects*: Equivalent to:
>
> ``` sourceCode
> return reflect_constant([: R :]);
> ```
>
> \[ *Example 2:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> constexpr int x = 0;
> constexpr int y = 0;
>
> static_assert(^^x != ^^y);                              // OK, x and y are different variables so their
>                                                         // reflections compare different
> static_assert(constant_of(^^x) == constant_of(^^y));    // OK, both constant_of(^^x) and constant_of(^^y)
>                                                         // represent the value 0
> static_assert(constant_of(^^x) == reflect_constant(0)); // OK, likewise
>
> struct S { int m; };
> constexpr S s {42};
> static_assert(is_object(constant_of(^^s)) &&
>               is_object(reflect_object(s)));
> static_assert(constant_of(^^s) != reflect_object(s));   // OK, template parameter object that is
>                                                         // template-argument-equivalent to s is a different
>                                                         // object than s
> static_assert(constant_of(^^s) ==
>               constant_of(reflect_object(s)));          // OK
>
> consteval info fn() {
>   constexpr int x = 42;
>   return ^^x;
> }
> constexpr info r = constant_of(fn());  // error: x is outside its lifetime
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_public(info r);
> consteval bool is_protected(info r);
> consteval bool is_private(info r);
> ```
>
> [9](#pnum_548) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents either
>
> - [(9.1)](#pnum_549) a class member or unnamed bit-field that is public, protected, or private, respectively, or
> - [(9.2)](#pnum_550) a direct base class relationship (*`D`*, *`B`*) for which *`B`* is, respectively, a public, protected, or private base class of *`D`*.
>
> Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_virtual(info r);
> ```
>
> [10](#pnum_551) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents either a virtual member function or a direct base class relationship (*`D`*, *`B`*) for which *`B`* is a virtual base class of *`D`*. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_pure_virtual(info r);
> consteval bool is_override(info r);
> ```
>
> [11](#pnum_552) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a member function that is pure virtual or overrides another member function, respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_final(info r);
> ```
>
> [12](#pnum_553) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a final class or a final member function. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_deleted(info r);
> consteval bool is_defaulted(info r);
> ```
>
> [13](#pnum_554) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a function that is a deleted function (\[dcl.fct.def.delete\]) or defaulted function (\[dcl.fct.def.default\]), respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_user_provided(info r);
> consteval bool is_user_declared(info r);
> ```
>
> [14](#pnum_555) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a function that is user-provided or user-declared (\[dcl.fct.def.default\]), respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_explicit(info r);
> ```
>
> [15](#pnum_556) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a member function that is declared explicit. Otherwise, `false`. \[ *Note 1:* If `r` represents a member function template that is declared `explicit`, `is_explicit``(``r``)` is still `false` because in general such queries for templates cannot be answered. — *end note* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_noexcept(info r);
> ```
>
> [16](#pnum_557) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a `noexcept` function type or a function with a non-throwing exception specification (\[except.spec\]). Otherwise, `false`. \[ *Note 2:* If `r` represents a function template that is declared `noexcept`, `is_noexcept``(``r``)` is still `false` because in general such queries for templates cannot be answered. — *end note* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_bit_field(info r);
> ```
>
> [17](#pnum_558) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a bit-field, or if `r` represents a data member description (*`T`*, *`N`*, *`A`*, *`W`*, *`NUA`*) (\[class.mem.general\]) for which *`W`* is not ⊥. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_enumerator(info r);
> ```
>
> [18](#pnum_559) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents an enumerator. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_const(info r);
> consteval bool is_volatile(info r);
> ```
>
> [19](#pnum_560) Let *`T`* be `type_of``(``r``)` if *`has-type`*`(``r``)` is `true`. Otherwise, let *`T`* be `dealias``(``r``)`.
>
> [20](#pnum_561) *Returns*: `true` if *`T`* represents a const or volatile type, respectively, or a const- or volatile-qualified function type, respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_mutable_member(info r);
> ```
>
> [21](#pnum_562) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a `mutable` non-static data member. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_lvalue_reference_qualified(info r);
> consteval bool is_rvalue_reference_qualified(info r);
> ```
>
> [22](#pnum_563) Let *`T`* be `type_of``(``r``)` if *`has-type`*`(``r``)` is `true`. Otherwise, let *`T`* be `dealias``(``r``)`.
>
> [23](#pnum_564) *Returns*: `true` if *`T`* represents a lvalue- or rvalue-reference qualified function type, respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool has_static_storage_duration(info r);
> consteval bool has_thread_storage_duration(info r);
> consteval bool has_automatic_storage_duration(info r);
> ```
>
> [24](#pnum_565) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents an object or variable that has static, thread, or automatic storage duration, respectively (\[basic.stc\]). Otherwise, `false`. \[ *Note 3:* It is not possible to have a reflection representing an object or variable having dynamic storage duration. — *end note* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool has_internal_linkage(info r);
> consteval bool has_module_linkage(info r);
> consteval bool has_external_linkage(info r);
> consteval bool has_c_language_linkage(info r);
> consteval bool has_linkage(info r);
> ```
>
> [25](#pnum_566) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a variable, function, type, template, or namespace whose name has internal linkage, module linkage, external linkage, C language linkage, or any linkage, respectively (\[basic.link\]). Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_complete_type(info r);
> ```
>
> [26](#pnum_567) *Returns*: `true` if `is_type``(``r``)` is `true` and there is some point in the evaluation context from which the type represented by `dealias``(``r``)` is not an incomplete type (\[basic.types\]). Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_enumerable_type(info r);
> ```
>
> [27](#pnum_568) A type *`T`* is *enumerable* from a point *`P`* if either
>
> - [(27.1)](#pnum_569) *`T`* is a class type complete at *`P`* or
> - [(27.2)](#pnum_570) *`T`* is an enumeration type defined by a declaration *`D`* such that *`D`* is reachable from *`P`* but *`P`* does not occur within an *`enum-specifier`* of *`D`* (\[dcl.enum\]).
>
> [28](#pnum_571) *Returns*: `true` if `dealias``(``r``)` represents a type that is enumerable from some point in the evaluation context. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> \[ *Example 3:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> class S;
> enum class E;
> static_assert(!is_enumerable_type(^^S));
> static_assert(!is_enumerable_type(^^E));
>
> class S {
>   void mfn() {
>     static_assert(is_enumerable_type(^^S));
>   }
>   static_assert(!is_enumerable_type(^^S));
> };
> static_assert(is_enumerable_type(^^S));
>
> enum class E {
>   A = is_enumerable_type(^^E) ? 1 : 2
> };
> static_assert(is_enumerable_type(^^E));
> static_assert(static_cast<int>(E::A) == 2);
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_variable(info r);
> ```
>
> [29](#pnum_572) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a variable. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_type(info r);
> consteval bool is_namespace(info r);
> ```
>
> [30](#pnum_573) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents an entity whose underlying entity is a type or namespace, respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_type_alias(info r);
> consteval bool is_namespace_alias(info r);
> ```
>
> [31](#pnum_574) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a type alias or namespace alias, respectively \[ *Note 4:* A specialization of an alias template is a type alias — *end note* \]. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_function(info r);
> ```
>
> [32](#pnum_575) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a function. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_conversion_function(info r);
> consteval bool is_operator_function(info r);
> consteval bool is_literal_operator(info r);
> ```
>
> [33](#pnum_576) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a function that is a conversion function (\[class.conv.fct\]), operator function (\[over.oper\]), or literal operator (\[over.literal\]), respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_special_member_function(info r);
> consteval bool is_constructor(info r);
> consteval bool is_default_constructor(info r);
> consteval bool is_copy_constructor(info r);
> consteval bool is_move_constructor(info r);
> consteval bool is_assignment(info r);
> consteval bool is_copy_assignment(info r);
> consteval bool is_move_assignment(info r);
> consteval bool is_destructor(info r);
> ```
>
> [34](#pnum_577) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a function that is a special member function (\[special\]), a constructor, a default constructor, a copy constructor, a move constructor, an assignment operator, a copy assignment operator, a move assignment operator, or a destructor, respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_template(info r);
> ```
>
> [35](#pnum_578) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a function template, class template, variable template, alias template, or concept. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> [36](#pnum_579) \[ *Note 5:* A template specialization is not a template. `is_template``(^^``std``::``vector``)` is `true` but `is_template``(^^``std``::``vector``<``int``>)` is `false`. — *end note* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_function_template(info r);
> consteval bool is_variable_template(info r);
> consteval bool is_class_template(info r);
> consteval bool is_alias_template(info r);
> consteval bool is_conversion_function_template(info r);
> consteval bool is_operator_function_template(info r);
> consteval bool is_literal_operator_template(info r);
> consteval bool is_constructor_template(info r);
> consteval bool is_concept(info r);
> ```
>
> [37](#pnum_580) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a function template, variable template, class template, alias template, conversion function template, operator function template, literal operator template, constructor template, or concept respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_value(info r);
> consteval bool is_object(info r);
> ```
>
> [38](#pnum_581) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a value or object, respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_structured_binding(info r);
> ```
>
> [39](#pnum_582) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a structured binding. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_class_member(info r);
> consteval bool is_namespace_member(info r);
> consteval bool is_nonstatic_data_member(info r);
> consteval bool is_static_member(info r);
> consteval bool is_base(info r);
> ```
>
> [40](#pnum_583) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a class member, namespace member, non-static data member, static member, or direct base class relationship, respectively. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool has_default_member_initializer(info r);
> ```
>
> [41](#pnum_584) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a non-static data member that has a default member initializer. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool has_parent(info r);
> ```
>
> [42](#pnum_585) *Returns*:
>
> - [(42.1)](#pnum_586) If `r` represents the global namespace, then `false`.
> - [(42.2)](#pnum_587) Otherwise, if `r` represents an entity that has C language linkage (\[dcl.link\]), then `false`.
> - [(42.3)](#pnum_588) Otherwise, if `r` represents an entity that has a language linkage other than C++ language linkage, then an implementation-defined value.
> - [(42.4)](#pnum_589) Otherwise, if `r` represents a type that is neither a class nor enumeration type, then `false`.
> - [(42.5)](#pnum_590) Otherwise, if `r` represents an entity or direct base class relationship, then `true`.
> - [(42.6)](#pnum_591) Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval info parent_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [43](#pnum_592) *Constant When*: `has_parent``(``r``)` is `true`.
>
> [44](#pnum_593) *Returns*:
>
> - [(44.1)](#pnum_594) If `r` represents a non-static data member that is a direct member of an anonymous union, or an unnamed bit-field declared within the *`member-specification`* of such a union, then a reflection representing the innermost enclosing anonymous union.
> - [(44.2)](#pnum_595) Otherwise, if `r` represents an enumerator, then a reflection representing the corresponding enumeration type.
> - [(44.3)](#pnum_596) Otherwise, if `r` represents a direct base class relationship (*`D`*, *`B`*), then a reflection representing *`D`*.
> - [(44.4)](#pnum_597) Otherwise, let *`E`* be the class, function, or namespace whose class scope, function parameter scope, or namespace scope, respectively, is the innermost such scope that either is, or encloses, the target scope of a declaration of what is represented by `r`.
>   - [(44.5)](#pnum_598) If *`E`* is the function call operator of a closure type for a *`consteval-block-declaration`* (\[dcl.pre\]), then `parent_of``(``parent_of``(^^`*`E`*`))`. \[ *Note 6:* In this case, the first `parent_of` will be the closure type, so the second `parent_of` is necessary to give the parent of that closure type. — *end note* \]
>   - [(44.6)](#pnum_599) Otherwise, `^^`*`E`*.
>
> \[ *Example 4:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> struct I { };
>
> struct F : I {
>   union {
>     int o;
>   };
>
>   enum N {
>     A
>   };
> };
>
> constexpr auto ctx = std::meta::access_context::current();
>
> static_assert(parent_of(^^F) == ^^::);
> static_assert(parent_of(bases_of(^^F, ctx)[0]) == ^^F);
> static_assert(is_union_type(parent_of(^^F::o)));
> static_assert(parent_of(^^F::N) == ^^F);
> static_assert(parent_of(^^F::A) == ^^F::N);
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval info dealias(info r);
> ```
>
> [45](#pnum_600) *Constant When*: `r` represents an entity.
>
> [46](#pnum_601) *Returns*: A reflection representing the underlying entity of what `r` represents.
>
> [47](#pnum_602)
>
> \[ *Example 5:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> using X = int;
> using Y = X;
> static_assert(dealias(^^int) == ^^int);
> static_assert(dealias(^^X) == ^^int);
> static_assert(dealias(^^Y) == ^^int);
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool has_template_arguments(info r);
> ```
>
> [48](#pnum_603) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a specialization of a function template, variable template, class template, or an alias template. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval info template_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [49](#pnum_604) *Constant When*: `has_template_arguments``(``r``)` is `true`.
>
> [50](#pnum_605) *Returns*: A reflection of the template of the specialization represented by `r`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval vector<info> template_arguments_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [51](#pnum_606) *Constant When*: `has_template_arguments``(``r``)` is `true`.
>
> [52](#pnum_607) *Returns*: A `vector` containing reflections of the template arguments of the template specialization represented by `r`, in the order they appear in the corresponding template argument list. For a given template argument *`A`*, its corresponding reflection *`R`* is determined as follows:
>
> - [(52.1)](#pnum_608) If *`A`* denotes a type or a type alias, then *`R`* is a reflection representing the underlying entity of *`A`*. \[ *Note 7:* *`R`* always represents a type, never a type alias. — *end note* \]
>
> - [(52.2)](#pnum_609) Otherwise, if *`A`* denotes a class template, variable template, concept, or alias template, then *`R`* is a reflection representing *`A`*.
>
> - [(52.3)](#pnum_610) Otherwise, *`A`* is a constant template argument (\[temp.arg.nontype\]). Let *`P`* be the corresponding template parameter.
>
>   - [(52.3.1)](#pnum_611) If *`P`* has reference type, then *`R`* is a reflection representing the object or function referred to by *`A`*.
>   - [(52.3.2)](#pnum_612) Otherwise, if *`P`* has class type, then *`R`* represents the corresponding template parameter object.
>   - [(52.3.3)](#pnum_613) Otherwise, *`R`* is a reflection representing the value computed by *`A`*.
>
> \[ *Example 6:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <class T, class U=T> struct Pair { };
> template <class T> struct Pair<char, T> { };
> template <class T> using PairPtr = Pair<T*>;
>
> static_assert(template_of(^^Pair<int>) == ^^Pair);
> static_assert(template_of(^^Pair<char, char>) == ^^Pair);
> static_assert(template_arguments_of(^^Pair<int>).size() == 2);
> static_assert(template_arguments_of(^^Pair<int>)[0] == ^^int);
>
> static_assert(template_of(^^PairPtr<int>) == ^^PairPtr);
> static_assert(template_arguments_of(^^PairPtr<int>).size() == 1);
>
> struct S { };
> int i;
> template <int, int&, S, template <class> class>
> struct X { };
> constexpr auto T = ^^X<1, i, S{}, PairPtr>;
> static_assert(is_value(template_arguments_of(T)[0]));
> static_assert(is_object(template_arguments_of(T)[1]));
> static_assert(is_object(template_arguments_of(T)[2]));
> static_assert(template_arguments_of(T)[3] == ^^PairPtr);
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]

### \[meta.reflection.access.context\] Access control context[](#meta.reflection.access.context-access-control-context)

> [1](#pnum_614) The `access_context` class is a non-aggregate type that represents a namespace, class, or function from which queries pertaining to access rules may be performed, as well as the designating class (\[class.access.base\]), if any.
>
> [2](#pnum_615) An `access_context` has an associated scope and designating class.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> struct access_context {
>    access_context() = delete;
>
>    consteval info scope() const;
>    consteval info designating_class() const;
>
>    static consteval access_context current() noexcept;
>    static consteval access_context unprivileged() noexcept;
>    static consteval access_context unchecked() noexcept;
>    consteval access_context via(info cls) const;
> };
> ```
>
> [3](#pnum_616) `access_context` is a structural type. Two values `ac1` and `ac2` of type `access_context` are template-argument-equivalent (\[temp.type\]) if `ac1``.``scope``()` and `ac2``.``scope``()` are template-argument-equivalent and `ac1``.``designating_class``()` and `ac2``.``designating_class``()` are template-argument-equivalent.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval info scope() const;
> consteval info designating_class() const;
> ```
>
> [4](#pnum_617) *Returns*: The `access_context`’s associated scope and designating class, respectively.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> static consteval access_context current() noexcept;
> ```
>
> [5](#pnum_618) `current` is not an addressable function (\[namespace.std\]).
>
> [6](#pnum_619) Given a program point *`P`*, let *`eval-point`*`(`*`P`*`)` be the following program point:
>
> - [(6.1)](#pnum_620) If a potentially-evaluated subexpression (\[intro.execution\]) of a default member initializer *`I`* for a member of a class *`C`* (\[class.mem.general\]) appears at *`P`*, then a point determined as follows:
>   - [(6.1.1)](#pnum_621) If an aggregate initialization is using *`I`*, *`eval-point`*`(`*`Q`*`)`, where *`Q`* is the point at which that aggregate initialization appears.
>   - [(6.1.2)](#pnum_622) Otherwise, if an initialization by an inherited constructor (\[class.inhctor.init\]) is using *`I`*, a point whose immediate scope is the class scope corresponding to *`C`*.
>   - [(6.1.3)](#pnum_623) Otherwise, a point whose immediate scope is the function parameter scope corresponding to the constructor definition that is using *`I`*.
> - [(6.2)](#pnum_624) Otherwise, if a potentially-evaluated subexpression of a default argument (\[dcl.fct.default\]) appears at *`P`*, *`eval-point`*`(`*`Q`*`)`, where *`Q`* is the point at which the invocation of the function (\[expr.call\]) using that default argument appears.
> - [(6.3)](#pnum_625) Otherwise, if the immediate scope of *`P`* is a function parameter scope introduced by a declaration *`D`*, and *`P`* appears either before the locus of *`D`* or within the trailing *`requires-clause`* of *`D`*, a point whose immediate scope is the innermost scope enclosing the locus of *`D`* that is not a template parameter scope.
> - [(6.4)](#pnum_626) Otherwise, if the immediate scope of *`P`* is a function parameter scope introduced by a *`lambda-expression`* *`L`* whose *`lambda-introducer`* appears at point *`Q`*, and *`P`* appears either within the *`trailing-return-type`* or the trailing *`requires-clause`* of *`L`*, *`eval-point`*`(`*`Q`*`)`.
> - [(6.5)](#pnum_627) Otherwise, if the innermost non-block scope enclosing *`P`* is the function parameter scope introduced by a *`consteval-block-declaration`* (\[dcl.pre\]), a point whose immediate scope is that inhabited by the outermost *`consteval-block-declaration`* *`D`* containing *`P`* such that each scope (if any) that intervenes between *`P`* and the function parameter scope introduced by *`D`* is either
>   - [(6.5.1)](#pnum_628) a block scope or
>   - [(6.5.2)](#pnum_629) a function parameter scope or lambda scope introduced by a *`consteval-block-declaration`*.
> - [(6.6)](#pnum_630) Otherwise, *`P`*.
>
> [7](#pnum_631) Given a scope *`S`*, let *`ctx-scope`*`(`*`S`*`)` be the following scope:
>
> - [(7.1)](#pnum_632) If *`S`* is a class scope or a namespace scope, *`S`*.
> - [(7.2)](#pnum_633) Otherwise, if *`S`* is a function parameter scope introduced by the declaration of a function, *`S`*.
> - [(7.3)](#pnum_634) Otherwise, if *`S`* is a lambda scope introduced by a *`lambda-expression`* *`L`*, the function parameter scope corresponding to the call operator of the closure type for *`L`*.
> - [(7.4)](#pnum_635) Otherwise, *`ctx-scope`*`(`*`S`*`')` where *`S`*`'` is the parent scope of *`S`*.
>
> [8](#pnum_636) An invocation of `current` that appears at a program point *`P`* is value-dependent (\[temp.dep.contexpr\]) if *`eval-point`*`(`*`P`*`)` is enclosed by a scope corresponding to a templated entity.
>
> [9](#pnum_637) *Returns*: An `access_context` whose designating class is the null reflection and whose scope represents the function, class, or namespace whose corresponding function parameter scope, class scope, or namespace scope is *`ctx-scope`*`(`*`S`*`)`, where *`S`* is the immediate scope of *`eval-point`*`(`*`P`*`)` and *`P`* is the point at which the invocation of `current` lexically appears.
>
> \[ *Example 1:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> struct A {
>   int a = 0;
>   consteval A(int p) : a(p) {}
> };
> struct B : A {
>   using A::A;
>   consteval B(int p, int q) : A(p * q) {}
>   info s = access_context::current().scope();
> };
> struct C : B { using B::B; };
>
> struct Agg {
>   consteval bool eq(info rhs = access_context::current().scope()) {
>     return s == rhs;
>   }
>   info s = access_context::current().scope();
> };
>
> namespace NS {
> static_assert(Agg{}.s == access_context::current().scope());  // OK
> static_assert(Agg{}.eq());  // OK
> static_assert(B(1).s == ^^B);  // OK
> static_assert(is_constructor(B{1, 2}.s) && parent_of(B{1, 2}.s) == ^^B);  // OK
> static_assert(is_constructor(C{1, 2}.s) && parent_of(C{1, 2}.s) == ^^B);  // OK
>
> auto fn() -> [:is_namespace(access_context::current().scope()) ? ^^int : ^^bool:];
> static_assert(type_of(^^fn) == ^^auto()->int);  // OK
>
> template <auto R>
> struct TCls {
>   consteval bool fn()
>     requires (is_type(access_context::current().scope())) {
>       // OK, scope is 'TCls<R>'.
>       return true;
>     }
> };
> static_assert(TCls<0>{}.fn());  // OK
> }
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> static consteval access_context unprivileged() noexcept;
> ```
>
> [10](#pnum_638) *Returns*: An `access_context` whose designating class is the null reflection and whose scope is the global namespace.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> static consteval access_context unchecked() noexcept;
> ```
>
> [11](#pnum_639) *Returns*: An `access_context` whose designating class and scope are both the null reflection.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval access_context via(info cls) const;
> ```
>
> [12](#pnum_640) *Constant When*: `cls` is either the null reflection or a reflection of a complete class type.
>
> [13](#pnum_641) *Returns*: An `access_context` whose scope is `this``->``scope``()` and whose designating class is `cls`.

### \[meta.reflection.access.queries\] Member accessibility queries[](#meta.reflection.access.queries-member-accessibility-queries)

> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_accessible(info r, access_context ctx);
> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_642) Let *`PARENT-CLS`*`(``r``)` be:
>
> - [(1.1)](#pnum_643) If `parent_of``(``r``)` represents a class *`C`*, then *`C`*.
> - [(1.2)](#pnum_644) Otherwise, *`PARENT-CLS`*`(``parent_of``(``r``))`.
>
> [2](#pnum_645) *Constant When*:
>
> - [(2.1)](#pnum_646) `r` does not represent a class member for which *`PARENT-CLS`*`(``r``)` is an incomplete class and
> - [(2.2)](#pnum_647) `r` does not represent a direct base class relationship (*`D`*, *`B`*) for which *`D`* is incomplete.
>
> [3](#pnum_648) Let *`DESIGNATING-CLS`*`(``r, ctx``)` be:
>
> - [(3.1)](#pnum_649) If `ctx``.``designating_class``()` represents a class *`C`*, then *`C`*.
> - [(3.2)](#pnum_650) Otherwise, *`PARENT-CLS`*`(``r``)`.
>
> [4](#pnum_651) *Returns*:
>
> - [(4.1)](#pnum_652) If `r` represents an unnamed bit-field *`F`*, then `is_accessible``(``r`_(*`H`*)`, ctx``)` where `r`_(*`H`*) represents a hypothetical non-static data member of the class represented by *`PARENT-CLS`*`(``r``)` with the same access as *`F`*. \[ *Note 1:* Unnamed bit-fields are treated as class members for the purpose of `is_accessible`. — *end note* \]
>
> - [(4.2)](#pnum_653) Otherwise, if `r` does not represent a class member or a direct base class relationship, then `true`.
>
> - [(4.3)](#pnum_654) Otherwise, if `r` represents
>
>   - [(4.3.1)](#pnum_655) a class member that is not a (possibly indirect or variant) member of *`DESIGNATING-CLS`*`(``r, ctx``)` or
>   - [(4.3.2)](#pnum_656) a direct base class relationship such that `parent_of``(``r``)` does not represent *`DESIGNATING-CLS`*`(``r, ctx``)` or a (direct or indirect) base class thereof,
>
>   then `false`.
>
> - [(4.4)](#pnum_657) Otherwise, if `ctx``.``scope``()` is the null reflection, then `true`.
>
> - [(4.5)](#pnum_658) Otherwise, letting *`P`* be a program point whose immediate scope is the function parameter scope, class scope, or namespace scope corresponding to the function, class, or namespace represented by `ctx``.``scope``()`:
>
>   - [(4.5.1)](#pnum_659) If `r` represents a direct base class relationship (*`D`*, *`B`*), then `true` if base class *`B`* of *`DESIGNATING-CLS`*`(``r, ctx``)` is accessible at *`P`* (\[class.access.base\]); otherwise, `false`.
>   - [(4.5.2)](#pnum_660) Otherwise, `r` represents a class member *`M`*; `true` if *`M`* would be accessible at *`P`* with the designating class (\[class.access.base\]) as *`DESIGNATING-CLS`*`(``r, ctx``)` if the effect of any *`using-declaration`*s (\[namespace.udecl\]) were ignored. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> \[ *Note 2:* The definitions of when a class member or base class is accessible from a point *`P`* do not consider whether a declaration of that entity is reachable from *`P`*. — *end note* \]
>
> \[ *Example 1:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval access_context fn() {
>   return access_context::current();
> }
>
> class Cls {
>     int mem;
>     friend consteval access_context fn();
> public:
>     static constexpr auto r = ^^mem;
> };
>
> static_assert(is_accessible(Cls::r, fn()));                        // OK
> static_assert(!is_accessible(Cls::r, access_context::current()));  // OK
> static_assert(is_accessible(Cls::r, access_context::unchecked())); // OK
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool has_inaccessible_nonstatic_data_members(
>       info r,
>       access_context ctx);
> ```
>
> [5](#pnum_661) *Constant When*:
>
> - [(5.1)](#pnum_662) `nonstatic_data_members_of``(``r, access_context``::``unchecked``())` is a constant subexpression and
> - [(5.2)](#pnum_663) `r` does not represent a closure type.
>
> [6](#pnum_664) *Returns*: `true` if `is_accessible``(`*`R`*`, ctx``)` is `false` for any *`R`* in `nonstatic_data_members_of``(``r, access_context``::``unchecked``())`. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool has_inaccessible_bases(info r, access_context ctx);
> ```
>
> [7](#pnum_665) *Constant When*: `bases_of``(``r, access_context``::``unchecked``())` is a constant subexpression.
>
> [8](#pnum_666) *Returns*: `true` if `is_accessible``(`*`R`*`, ctx``)` is `false` for any *`R`* in `bases_of``(``r, access_context``::``unchecked``())`. Otherwise, `false`.

### \[meta.reflection.member.queries\] Reflection member queries[](#meta.reflection.member.queries-reflection-member-queries)

> ``` sourceCode
> consteval vector<info> members_of(info r, access_context ctx);
> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_667) *Constant When*: `dealias``(``r``)` is a reflection representing either a class type that is complete from some point in the evaluation context or a namespace.
>
> [2](#pnum_668) A declaration *`D`* *members-of-precedes* a point *`P`* if *`D`* precedes either *`P`* or the point immediately following the *`class-specifier`* of the outermost class for which *`P`* is in a complete-class context.
>
> [3](#pnum_669) A declaration *`D`* of a member *`M`* of a class or namespace *`Q`* is **`Q`*-members-of-eligible* if
>
> - [(3.1)](#pnum_670) the host scope of *`D`* (\[basic.scope.scope\]) is the class scope or namespace scope associated with *`Q`*,
> - [(3.2)](#pnum_671) *`D`* is not a friend declaration,
> - [(3.3)](#pnum_672) *`M`* is not a closure type (\[expr.prim.lambda.closure\]),
> - [(3.4)](#pnum_673) *`M`* is not a specialization of a template (\[temp.pre\]),
> - [(3.5)](#pnum_674) if *`Q`* is a class that is not a closure type, then *`M`* is a direct member of *`Q`* (\[class.mem.general\]) that is not a variant member of a nested anonymous union of *`Q`* (\[class.union.anon\]), and
> - [(3.6)](#pnum_675) if *`Q`* is a closure type, then *`M`* is a function call operator or function call operator template.
>
> It is implementation-defined whether declarations of other members of a closure type *`Q`* are *`Q`*-members-of-eligible.
>
> [4](#pnum_676) A member *`M`* of a class or namespace *`Q`* is **`Q`*-members-of-representable* from a point *`P`* if a *`Q`*-members-of-eligible declaration of *`M`* members-of-precedes *`P`* and *`M`* is
>
> - [(4.1)](#pnum_677) a class or enumeration type,
> - [(4.2)](#pnum_678) a type alias,
> - [(4.3)](#pnum_679) a class template, function template, variable template, alias template, or concept,
> - [(4.4)](#pnum_680) a variable or reference *`V`* for which the type of *`V`* does not contain an undeduced placeholder type,
> - [(4.5)](#pnum_681) a function *`F`* for which
>   - [(4.6)](#pnum_682) the type of *`F`* does not contain an undeduced placeholder type,
>   - [(4.7)](#pnum_683) the constraints (if any) of *`F`* are satisfied, and
>   - [(4.8)](#pnum_684) if *`F`* is a prospective destructor, *`F`* is the selected destructor (\[class.dtor\]),
> - [(4.9)](#pnum_685) a non-static data member,
> - [(4.10)](#pnum_686) a namespace, or
> - [(4.11)](#pnum_687) a namespace alias.
>
> \[ *Note 1:* Examples of direct members that are not *`Q`*-members-of-representable for any entity *`Q`* include: unscoped enumerators (\[enum\]), partial specializations of templates (\[temp.spec.partial\]), and closure types (\[expr.prim.lambda.closure\]). — *end note* \]
>
> [5](#pnum_688) *Returns*: A `vector` containing reflections of all members *`M`* of the entity *`Q`* represented by `dealias``(``r``)` for which
>
> - [(5.1)](#pnum_689) *`M`* is *`Q`*-members-of-representable from some point in the evaluation context and
> - [(5.2)](#pnum_690) `is_accessible``(^^`*`M`*`, ctx``)` is `true`.
>
> If `dealias``(``r``)` represents a class *`C`*, then the `vector` also contains reflections representing all unnamed bit-fields *`B`* whose declarations inhabit the class scope corresponding to *`C`* for which `is_accessible``(^^`*`B`*`, ctx``)` is `true`. Reflections of class members and unnamed bit-fields that are declared appear in the order in which they are declared. \[ *Note 2:* Base classes are not members. Implicitly-declared special members appear after any user-declared members (\[special\]). — *end note* \]
>
> \[ *Example 1:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> // TU1
> export module M;
> namespace NS {
>   export int m;
>   static int l;
> }
> static_assert(members_of(^^NS, access_context::current()).size() == 2);
>
> // TU2
> import M;
>
> static_assert(members_of(^^NS, access_context::current()).size() == 1);
>   // NS::l does not precede the constant-expression ([basic.lookup])
>
> class B {};
>
> struct S : B {
> private:
>   class I;
> public:
>   int m;
> };
>
> static_assert(members_of(^^S, access_context::current()).size() == 7);    // 6 special members, 1 public member, does not include base
> static_assert(members_of(^^S, access_context::unchecked()).size() == 8);  // all of the above, as well a reflection representing S::I
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval vector<info> bases_of(info type, access_context ctx);
> ```
>
> [6](#pnum_691) *Constant When*: `dealias``(``type``)` represents a class type that is complete from some point in the evaluation context.
>
> [7](#pnum_692) *Returns*: Let *`C`* be the class represented by `dealias``(``type``)`. A `vector` containing the reflections of all the direct base class relationships *`B`*, if any, of *`C`* such that `is_accessible``(^^`*`B`*`, ctx``)` is `true`. The direct base class relationships appear in the order in which the corresponding base classes appear in the *`base-specifier-list`* of *`C`*.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval vector<info> static_data_members_of(info type, access_context ctx);
> ```
>
> [8](#pnum_693) *Constant When*: `dealias``(``type``)` represents a class type that is complete from some point in the evaluation context.
>
> [9](#pnum_694) *Returns*: A `vector` containing each element `e` of `members_of``(``type, ctx``)` such that `is_variable``(``e``)` is `true`, preserving their order.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval vector<info> nonstatic_data_members_of(info type, access_context ctx);
> ```
>
> [10](#pnum_695) *Constant When*: `dealias``(``type``)` represents a class type that is complete from some point in the evaluation context.
>
> [11](#pnum_696) *Returns*: A `vector` containing each element `e` of `members_of``(``type, ctx``)` such that `is_nonstatic_data_member``(``e``)` is `true`, preserving their order.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval vector<info> enumerators_of(info type_enum);
> ```
>
> [12](#pnum_697) *Constant When*: `dealias``(``type_enum``)` represents an enumeration type and `is_enumerable_type``(``type_enum``)` is `true`.
>
> [13](#pnum_698) *Returns*: A `vector` containing the reflections of each enumerator of the enumeration represented by `dealias``(``type_enum``)`, in the order in which they are declared.

### \[meta.reflection.layout\] Reflection layout queries[](#meta.reflection.layout-reflection-layout-queries)

> ``` sourceCode
> struct member_offset {
>   ptrdiff_t bytes;
>   ptrdiff_t bits;
>   constexpr ptrdiff_t total_bits() const;
>   auto operator<=>(const member_offset&) const = default;
> };
>
> constexpr ptrdiff_t member_offset::total_bits() const;
> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_699) *Returns*: `bytes ``*`` CHAR_BIT ``+`` bits`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval member_offset offset_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [2](#pnum_700) *Constant When*: `r` represents a non-static data member, unnamed bit-field, or direct base class relationship (*`D`*, *`B`*) for which either *`B`* is not a virtual base class or *`D`* is not an abstract class.
>
> [3](#pnum_701) Let *`V`* be the offset in bits from the beginning of a complete object of type `parent_of``(``r``)` to the subobject associated with the entity represented by `r`.
>
> [4](#pnum_702) *Returns*: `{`*`V`*` ``/`` CHAR_BIT, `*`V`*` ``%`` CHAR_BIT``}`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval size_t size_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [5](#pnum_703) *Constant When*: `dealias``(``r``)` is a reflection of a type, object, value, variable of non-reference type, non-static data member that is not a bit-field, direct base class relationship, or data member description (*`T`*, *`N`*, *`A`*, *`W`*, *`NUA`*) (\[class.mem.general\]) where *`W`* is not ⊥. If `dealias``(``r``)` represents a type, then `is_complete_type``(``r``)` is `true`.
>
> [6](#pnum_704) *Returns*: If `r` represents
>
> - [(6.1)](#pnum_705) a non-static data member of type *`T`*,
> - [(6.2)](#pnum_706) a data member description (*`T`*, *`N`*, *`A`*, *`W`*, *`NUA`*), or
> - [(6.3)](#pnum_707) `dealias``(``r``)` represents a type *`T`*,
>
> then `sizeof``(`*`T`*`)` if *`T`* is not a reference type and `size_of``(``add_pointer``(^^`*`T`*`))` otherwise. Otherwise, `size_of``(``type_of``(``r``))`.
>
> \[ *Note 1:* It is possible that while `sizeof``(``char``)`` ``==`` size_of``(^^``char``)` that `sizeof``(``char``&)`` ``!=`` size_of``(^^``char``&)`. If `b` represents a direct base class relationship (*`D`*, *`B`*) for which *`B`* is an empty class type, then `size_of``(``b``)`` ``>`` ``0`. — *end note* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval size_t alignment_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [7](#pnum_708) *Constant When*: `dealias``(``r``)` is a reflection of a type, object, variable of non-reference type, non-static data member that is not a bit-field, direct base class relationship, or data member description. If `dealias``(``r``)` represents a type, then `is_complete_type``(``r``)` is `true`.
>
> [8](#pnum_709) *Returns*:
>
> - [(8.1)](#pnum_710) If `dealias``(``r``)` represents a type *`T`*, then `alignment_of``(``add_pointer``(``r``))` if *`T`* is a reference type and the alignment requirement of *`T`* otherwise.
> - [(8.2)](#pnum_711) Otherwise, if `dealias``(``r``)` represents a variable or object, then the alignment requirement of the variable or object.
> - [(8.3)](#pnum_712) Otherwise, if `r` represents a direct base class relationship, then `alignment_of``(``type_of``(``r``))`.
> - [(8.4)](#pnum_713) Otherwise, if `r` represents a non-static data member *`M`* of a class *`C`*, then the alignment of the direct member subobject corresponding to *`M`* of a complete object of type *`C`*.
> - [(8.5)](#pnum_714) Otherwise, `r` represents a data member description (*`T`*, *`N`*, *`A`*, *`W`*, *`NUA`*) (\[class.mem.general\]). If *`A`* is not ⊥, then the value *`A`*. Otherwise, `alignment_of``(^^`*`T`*`)`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval size_t bit_size_of(info r);
> ```
>
> [9](#pnum_715) *Constant When*: `dealias``(``r``)` is a reflection of a type, object, value, variable of non-reference type, non-static data member, unnamed bit-field, direct base class relationship, or data member description. If `dealias``(``r``)` represents a type *`T`*, there is a point within the evaluation context from which *`T`* is not incomplete.
>
> [10](#pnum_716) *Returns*:
>
> - [(10.1)](#pnum_717) If `r` represents a non-static data member that is a bit-field or an unnamed bit-field with width *`W`*, then *`W`*.
> - [(10.2)](#pnum_718) Otherwise, if `r` represents a data member description (*`T`*, *`N`*, *`A`*, *`W`*, *`NUA`*) (\[class.mem.general\]) and *`W`* is not ⊥, then *`W`*.
> - [(10.3)](#pnum_719) Otherwise, `CHAR_BIT ``*`` size_of``(``r``)`.

### \[meta.reflection.extract\] Value extraction[](#meta.reflection.extract-value-extraction)

> [1](#pnum_720) The `extract` function template may be used to extract a value out of a reflection when its type is known.
>
> [2](#pnum_721) The following are defined for exposition only to aid in the specification of `extract`:
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <class T>
>   consteval T extract-ref(info r); // exposition only
> ```
>
> [3](#pnum_722) \[ *Note 1:* `T` is a reference type. — *end note* \]
>
> [4](#pnum_723) *Constant When*:
>
> - [(4.1)](#pnum_724) `r` represents a variable or object of type `U`,
> - [(4.2)](#pnum_725) `is_convertible_v``<``remove_reference_t``<``U``>(*)[]``, remove_reference_t``<``T``>(*)[]>` is `true`, and \[ *Note 2:* The intent is to allow only qualification conversions from `U` to `T`. — *end note* \]
> - [(4.3)](#pnum_726) if `r` represents a variable, then either that variable is usable in constant expressions or its lifetime began within the core constant expression currently under evaluation.
>
> [5](#pnum_727) *Returns*: If `r` represents an object *`O`*, then a reference to *`O`*. Otherwise, a reference to the object declared, or referred to, by the variable represented by `r`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <class T>
>   consteval T extract-member-or-function(info r); // exposition only
> ```
>
> [6](#pnum_728) *Constant When*:
>
> - [(6.1)](#pnum_729) `r` represents a non-static data member with type `X`, that is not a bit-field, that is a direct member of a class `C`, `T` and `X C``::*` are similar types (\[conv.qual\]), and `is_convertible_v``<``X C``::*``, T``>` is `true`;
> - [(6.2)](#pnum_730) `r` represents an implicit object member function with type `F` or `F ``noexcept` that is a direct member of a class `C` and `T` is `F C``::*`; or
> - [(6.3)](#pnum_731) `r` represents a non-member function, static member function, or explicit object member function of function type `F` or `F ``noexcept` and `T` is `F``*`.
>
> [7](#pnum_732) *Returns*:
>
> - [(7.1)](#pnum_733) If `T` is a pointer type, then a pointer value pointing to the function represented by `r`.
> - [(7.2)](#pnum_734) Otherwise, a pointer-to-member value designating the non-static data member or function represented by `r`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <class T>
>   consteval T extract-value(info r); // exposition only
> ```
>
> [8](#pnum_735) Let `U` be the type of the value or object that `r` represents.
>
> [9](#pnum_736) *Constant When*:
>
> - [(9.1)](#pnum_737) `U` is a pointer type, `T` and `U` are either similar or both function pointer types, and `is_convertible_v``<``U, T``>` is `true`,
> - [(9.2)](#pnum_738) `U` is not a pointer type and the cv-unqualified types of `T` and `U` are the same,
> - [(9.3)](#pnum_739) `U` is an array type, `T` is a pointer type, and the value that `r` represents is convertible to `T`, or
> - [(9.4)](#pnum_740) `U` is a closure type, `T` is a function pointer type, and the value that `r` represents is convertible to `T`.
>
> [10](#pnum_741) *Returns*: `static_cast``<``T``>([:`*`R`*`:])`, where *`R`* is a constant expression of type `info` such that *`R`*` ``==`` r` is `true`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <class T>
>   consteval T extract(info r);
> ```
>
> [11](#pnum_742) *Effects*: Let `U` be `remove_cv_t``<``T``>`. Equivalent to:
>
> ``` sourceCode
> if constexpr (is_reference_type(^^T)) {
>   return extract-ref<T>(r);
> } else if constexpr (is_nonstatic_data_member(r) || is_function(r)) {
>   return extract-member-or-function<U>(r);
> } else {
>   return extract-value<U>(constant_of(r));
> }
> ```

### \[meta.reflection.substitute\] Reflection substitution[](#meta.reflection.substitute-reflection-substitution)

> ``` sourceCode
> template <class R>
> concept reflection_range =
>   ranges::input_range<R> &&
>   same_as<ranges::range_value_t<R>, info> &&
>   same_as<remove_cvref_t<ranges::range_reference_t<R>>, info>;
> ```
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <reflection_range R = initializer_list<info>>
> consteval bool can_substitute(info templ, R&& arguments);
> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_743) *Constant When*: `templ` represents a template and every reflection in `arguments` represents a construct usable as a template argument (\[temp.arg\]).
>
> [2](#pnum_744) Let `Z` be the template represented by `templ` and let `Args``...` be a sequence of prvalue constant expressions that compute the reflections held by the elements of `arguments`, in order.
>
> [3](#pnum_745) *Returns*: `true` if `Z``<[:``Args``:]...>` is a valid *`template-id`* (\[temp.names\]) that does not name a function whose type contains an undeduced placeholder type. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> [4](#pnum_746) \[ *Note 1:* If forming `Z``<[:``Args``:]...>` leads to a failure outside of the immediate context, the program is ill-formed. — *end note* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <reflection_range R = initializer_list<info>>
> consteval info substitute(info templ, R&& arguments);
> ```
>
> [5](#pnum_747) *Constant When*: `can_substitute``(``templ, arguments``)` is `true`.
>
> [6](#pnum_748) Let `Z` be the template represented by `templ` and let `Args``...` be a sequence of prvalue constant expressions that compute the reflections held by the elements of `arguments`, in order.
>
> [7](#pnum_749) *Returns*: `^^``Z``<[:``Args``:]...>`.
>
> [8](#pnum_750) \[ *Note 2:* If forming `Z``<[:``Args``:]...>` leads to a failure outside of the immediate context, the program is ill-formed. — *end note* \]
>
> [9](#pnum_751)
>
> \[ *Example 1:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <typename T>
> auto fn1();
>
> static_assert(!can_substitute(^^fn1, {^^int}));  // OK
> constexpr info r1 = substitute(^^fn1, {^^int});
>   // error: fn<int> contains an undeduced placeholder type
>
> template <typename T>
> auto fn2() {
>   static_assert(^^T != ^^int);
>     // static assertion failed during instantiation of fn<int>
>   return 0;
> }
>
> constexpr bool r2 = can_substitute(^^fn2, {^^int});
>   // error: instantiation of body of fn<int> is needed to deduce return type
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> [10](#pnum_752)
>
> \[ *Example 2:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval info to_integral_constant(unsigned i) {
>   return substitute(^^integral_constant, {^^unsigned, reflect_constant(i)});
> }
> constexpr info r = to_integral_constant(2);
>   // OK, r represents the type integral_constant<unsigned, 2>
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]

### \[meta.reflection.result\] Expression result reflection[](#meta.reflection.result-expression-result-reflection)

> ``` sourceCode
> template <typename T>
>   consteval info reflect_constant(T expr);
> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_753) *Mandates*: `is_copy_constructible_v``<``T``>` is `true` and `T` is a cv-unqualified structural type (\[temp.param\]) that is not a reference type.
>
> [2](#pnum_754) Let *`V`* be:
>
> - [(2.1)](#pnum_755) if `T` is a class type, then an object that is template-argument-equivalent to the value of `expr`;
> - [(2.2)](#pnum_756) otherwise, the value of `expr`.
>
> [3](#pnum_757) *Constant When*: Given the invented template
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <T P> struct TCls;
> ```
>
> the *`template-id`* `TCls``<`*`V`*`>` would be valid.
>
> [4](#pnum_758) *Returns*: `template_arguments_of``(^^``TCls``<`*`V`*`>)[``0``]`. \[ *Note 1:* This is a reflection of an object for class types and a reflection of a value otherwise. — *end note* \]
>
> \[ *Example 1:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <auto D>
> struct A { };
>
> struct N { int x; };
> struct K { char const* p; };
>
> constexpr info r1 = reflect_constant(42);
> static_assert(is_value(r1));
> static_assert(r1 == template_arguments_of(^^A<42>)[0]);
>
> constexpr info r2 = reflect_constant(N{42});
> static_assert(is_object(r2));
> static_assert(r2 == template_arguments_of(^^A<N{42}>)[0]);
>
> constexpr info r3 = reflect_constant(K{nullptr}); // ok
> constexpr info r4 = reflect_constant(K{"ebab"});  // error: constituent pointer points to string literal
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <typename T>
>   consteval info reflect_object(T& expr);
> ```
>
> [5](#pnum_759) *Mandates*: `T` is an object type.
>
> [6](#pnum_760) *Constant When*: `expr` is suitable for use as a constant template argument for a constant template parameter of type `T``&` (\[temp.arg.nontype\]).
>
> [7](#pnum_761) *Returns*: A reflection of the object designated by `expr`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <typename T>
>   consteval info reflect_function(T& fn);
> ```
>
> [8](#pnum_762) *Mandates*: `T` is a function type.
>
> [9](#pnum_763) *Constant When*: `fn` is suitable for use as a constant template argument for a constant template parameter of type `T``&` (\[temp.arg.nontype\]).
>
> [10](#pnum_764) *Returns*: A reflection of the function designated by `fn`.

### \[meta.reflection.define.aggregate\] Reflection class definition generation[](#meta.reflection.define.aggregate-reflection-class-definition-generation)

> ``` sourceCode
> struct data_member_options {
>   struct name-type { // exposition only
>     template<class T> requires constructible_from<u8string, T>
>       consteval name-type(T &&);
>
>     template<class T> requires constructible_from<string, T>
>       consteval name-type(T &&);
>
>   private:
>     variant<u8string, string> contents;    // exposition only
>   };
>
>   optional<name-type> name;
>   optional<int> alignment;
>   optional<int> bit_width;
>   bool no_unique_address = false;
> };
> ```
>
> [1](#pnum_765) The classes `data_member_options` and `data_member_options``::`*`name-type`* are consteval-only types (\[basic.types.general\]), and are not structural types (\[temp.param\]).
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template <class T> requires constructible_from<u8string, T>
> consteval data_member_options::name-type(T&& value);
> ```
>
> [2](#pnum_766) *Effects*: Initializes *`contents`* with `u8string``(``std``::``forward``<``T``>(``value``))`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> template<class T> requires constructible_from<string, T>
> consteval data_member_options::name-type(T&& value);
> ```
>
> [3](#pnum_767) *Effects*: Initializes *`contents`* with `string``(``std``::``forward``<``T``>(``value``))`.
>
> \[ *Note 1:* The class *`name-type`* allows the function `data_member_spec` to accept an ordinary string literal (or `string_view`, `string`, etc.) or a UTF-8 string literal (or `u8string_view`, `u8string`, etc.) equally well.
>
> \[ *Example 1:*
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval void fn() {
>   data_member_options o1 = {.name="ordinary_literal_encoding"};
>   data_member_options o2 = {.name=u8"utf8_encoding"};
> }
> ```
>
> — *end example* \]
>
> — *end note* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval info data_member_spec(info type,
>                                 data_member_options options);
> ```
>
> [4](#pnum_768) *Constant When*:
>
> - [(4.1)](#pnum_769) `dealias``(``type``)` represents either an object type or a reference type;
> - [(4.2)](#pnum_770) if `options``.``name` contains a value, then:
>   - [(4.2.1)](#pnum_771) `holds_alternative``<``u8string``>(``options``.``name``->`*`contents`*`)` is `true` and `get``<``u8string``>(``options``.``name``->`*`contents`*`)` contains a valid identifier (\[lex.name\]) that is not a keyword (\[lex.key\]) when interpreted with UTF-8, or
>   - [(4.2.2)](#pnum_772) `holds_alternative``<``string``>(``options``.``name``->`*`contents`*`)` is `true` and `get``<``string``>(``options``.``name``->`*`contents`*`)` contains a valid identifier that is not a keyword when interpreted with the ordinary literal encoding;
>
>   \[ *Note 2:* The name corresponds to the spelling of an identifier token after phase 6 of translation (\[lex.phases\]). Lexical constructs like *`universal-character-name`*s \[lex.universal.char\] are not processed and will cause evaluation to fail. For example, `R"(\u03B1)"` is an invalid identifier and is not interpreted as `"α"`. — *end note* \]
> - [(4.3)](#pnum_773) if `options``.``name` does not contain a value, then `options``.``bit_width` contains a value;
> - [(4.4)](#pnum_774) if `options``.``bit_width` contains a value *`V`*, then
>   - [(4.4.1)](#pnum_775) `is_integral_type``(``type``)`` ``||`` is_enumeration_type``(``type``)` is `true`,
>   - [(4.4.2)](#pnum_776) `options``.``alignment` does not contain a value,
>   - [(4.4.3)](#pnum_777) `options``.``no_unique_address` is `false`, and
>   - [(4.4.4)](#pnum_778) if *`V`* equals `0` then `options``.``name` does not contain a value; and
> - [(4.5)](#pnum_779) if `options``.``alignment` contains a value, it is an alignment value (\[basic.align\]) not less than `alignment_of``(``type``)`.
>
> [5](#pnum_780) *Returns*: A reflection of a data member description (*`T`*, *`N`*, *`A`*, *`W`*, *`NUA`*) (\[class.mem.general\]) where
>
> - [(5.1)](#pnum_781) *`T`* is the type represented by `dealias``(``type``)`,
> - [(5.2)](#pnum_782) *`N`* is either the identifier encoded by `options``.``name` or ⊥ if `options``.``name` does not contain a value,
> - [(5.3)](#pnum_783) *`A`* is either the alignment value held by `options``.``alignment` or ⊥ if `options``.``alignment` does not contain a value,
> - [(5.4)](#pnum_784) *`W`* is either the value held by `options``.``bit_width` or ⊥ if `options``.``bit_width` does not contain a value, and
> - [(5.5)](#pnum_785) *`NUA`* is the value held by `options``.``no_unique_address`.
>
> [6](#pnum_786) \[ *Note 3:* The returned reflection value is primarily useful in conjunction with `define_aggregate`; it can also be queried by certain other functions in `std``::``meta` (e.g., `type_of`, `identifier_of`). — *end note* \]
>
> ``` sourceCode
> consteval bool is_data_member_spec(info r);
> ```
>
> [7](#pnum_787) *Returns*: `true` if `r` represents a data member description. Otherwise, `false`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
>   template <reflection_range R = initializer_list<info>>
>   consteval info define_aggregate(info class_type, R&& mdescrs);
> ```
>
> [8](#pnum_788) Let *`C`* be the class represented by `class_type` and *`r`*_(*`K`*) be the *`K`*^(th) reflection value in `mdescrs`. For every *`r`*_(*`K`*) in `mdescrs`, let (*`T`*_(*`K`*), *`N`*_(*`K`*), *`A`*_(*`K`*), *`W`*_(*`K`*), *`NUA`*_(*`K`*)) be the corresponding data member description represented by *`r`*_(*`K`*).
>
> [9](#pnum_789) *Constant When*:
>
> - [(9.1)](#pnum_790) *`C`* is incomplete from every point in the evaluation context; \[ *Note 4:* *`C`* can be a class template specialization for which there is a reachable definition of the class template. In this case, the injected declaration is an explicit specialization. — *end note* \]
>
> - [(9.2)](#pnum_791) `is_data_member_spec``(`*`r`*_(*`K`*)`)` is `true` for every *`r`*_(*`K`*);
>
> - [(9.3)](#pnum_792) `is_complete_type``(`*`T`*_(*`K`*)`)` is `true` for every *`r`*_(*`K`*); and
>
> - [(9.4)](#pnum_793) for every pair (*`r`*_(*`K`*), *`r`*_(*`L`*)) where `K ``<`` L`, if *`N`*_(*`K`*) is not ⊥ and *`N`*_(*`L`*) is not ⊥, then either:
>
>   - [(9.4.1)](#pnum_794) *`N`*_(*`K`*)` ``!=`` `*`N`*_(*`L`*) is `true` or
>   - [(9.4.2)](#pnum_795) *`N`*_(*`K`*)` ``==`` ``u8"_"` is `true`. \[ *Note 5:* Every provided identifier is unique or `"_"`. — *end note* \]
>
> [10](#pnum_796) *Effects*: Produces an injected declaration *`D`* (\[expr.const\]) that defines *`C`* and has properties as follows:
>
> - [(10.1)](#pnum_797) The target scope of *`D`* is the scope to which *`C`* belongs (\[basic.scope.scope\]).
>
> - [(10.2)](#pnum_798) The locus of *`D`* follows immediately after the core constant expression currently under evaluation.
>
> - [(10.3)](#pnum_799) The characteristic sequence of *`D`* (\[expr.const\]) is the sequence of reflection values *`r`*_(*`K`*).
>
> - [(10.4)](#pnum_800) If *`C`* is a specialization of a templated class *`T`*, and *`C`* is not a local class, then *`D`* is an explicit specialization of *`T`*.
>
> - [(10.5)](#pnum_801) For each *`r`*_(*`K`*), there is a corresponding entity *`M`*_(*`K`*) belonging to the class scope of *`D`* with the following properties:
>
>   - [(10.5.1)](#pnum_802) If *`N`*_(*`K`*) is ⊥, *`M`*_(*`K`*) is an unnamed bit-field. Otherwise, *`M`*_(*`K`*) is a non-static data member whose name is the identifier determined by the character sequence encoded by *`N`*_(*`K`*) in UTF-8.
>   - [(10.5.2)](#pnum_803) The type of *`M`*_(*`K`*) is *`T`*_(*`K`*).
>   - [(10.5.3)](#pnum_804) *`M`*_(*`K`*) is declared with the attribute `[[``no_unique_address``]]` if and only if *`NUA`*_(*`K`*) is `true`.
>   - [(10.5.4)](#pnum_805) If *`W`*_(*`K`*) is not ⊥, *`M`*_(*`K`*) is a bit-field whose width is that value. Otherwise, *`M`*_(*`K`*) is not a bit-field.
>   - [(10.5.5)](#pnum_806) If *`A`*_(*`K`*) is not ⊥, *`M`*_(*`K`*) has the *`alignment-specifier`* `alignas``(`*`A`*_(*`K`*)`)`. Otherwise, *`M`*_(*`K`*) has no *`alignment-specifier`*.
>
> - For every *`r`*_(*`L`*) in `mdescrs` such that *`K`*` ``<`` `*`L`*, the declaration corresponding to *`r`*_(*`K`*) precedes the declaration corresponding to *`r`*_(*`L`*).
>
> [11](#pnum_807) *Returns*: `class_type`.

### \[meta.reflection.traits\] Reflection type traits[](#meta.reflection.traits-reflection-type-traits)

> [1](#pnum_808) Subclause \[meta.reflection.traits\] specifies consteval functions to query the properties of types (\[meta.unary\]), query the relationships between types (\[meta.rel\]), or transform types (\[meta.trans\]) at compile time. Each consteval function declared in this class has an associated class template declared elsewhere in this document.
>
> [2](#pnum_809) Every function and function template declared in this subclause has the following conditions required for a call to that function or function template to be a constant subexpression (\[defns.const.subexpr\]):
>
> - [(2.1)](#pnum_810) For every parameter `p` of type `info`, `is_type``(``p``)` is `true`.
> - [(2.2)](#pnum_811) For every parameter `r` whose type is constrained on `reflection_range`, `ranges``::``all_of``(``r, is_type``)` is `true`.
>
> ``` sourceCode
> // associated with [meta.unary.cat], primary type categories
> consteval bool is_void_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_null_pointer_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_integral_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_floating_point_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_array_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_pointer_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_lvalue_reference_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_rvalue_reference_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_member_object_pointer_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_member_function_pointer_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_enum_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_union_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_class_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_function_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_reflection_type(info type);
>
>   // associated with [meta.unary.comp], composite type categories
> consteval bool is_reference_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_arithmetic_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_fundamental_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_object_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_scalar_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_compound_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_member_pointer_type(info type);
>
> // associated with [meta.unary.prop], type properties
> consteval bool is_const_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_volatile_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_trivially_copyable_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_trivially_relocatable_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_replaceable_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_standard_layout_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_empty_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_polymorphic_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_abstract_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_final_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_aggregate_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_consteval_only_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_signed_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_unsigned_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_bounded_array_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_unbounded_array_type(info type);
> consteval bool is_scoped_enum_type(info type);
>
> template <reflection_range R = initializer_list<info>>
> consteval bool is_constructible_type(info type, R&& type_args);
> consteval bool is_default_constructible_type(info type);
